# Hardware Design Specification: AeroOptix-Horizon

| Property             | Value |
| -------------------- |---|
| Project              | AeroOptix-Horizon |
| Document owner       | Human engineering owner (TBD) |
| Document revision    | 0.79 |
| Last updated         | 2026-11-21 |
| Design tool          | KiCad 10 (saved files written by eeschema 10.0) |

**Conventions**

- `CONFIRMED`: the human owner confirmed the requirement or value.
- `NOT_VERIFIED`: no evidence exists yet. This applies to every requirement in this document unless a row says otherwise.
- `TBD`: to be decided. A value written as `TBD`, or left out, is open.
- A part shown in a diagram is a candidate only. It is not an approved part number.

**Part-number boundary:** Only `STM32H743VIT6`, `STM32F100C8T6B`, `ICM-45686` (2 × IMU, DEC-008), `ICP-20100` (barometer 1, DEC-010), `BMP581` (barometer 2, DEC-010; fallback BMP384), `IST8310` (iSentek magnetometer, DEC-011), `TPS62142RGTR` (main 3.3 V buck, DEC-012), `TPS2121` in the RUX package (5 V power mux, DEC-029; orderable code TPS2121RUXR or TPS2121RUXT not chosen), `TPS73533DRBR` (`+3V3A` LDO, DEC-027), `ECS-2520MV-160-CN-TR` (H743 16 MHz oscillator, DEC-048) and `ASDDV-24.000MHZ-LC-T` (F100 24 MHz oscillator, DEC-048) are confirmed part numbers. RC IN: buffer TI `SN74LVC1G34DCKR` and ESD diode Littelfuse `SP3031-01ETG` (DEC-057; DEC-056 named a 74LVC1G17). GPS1 buzzer line ESD diode: Littelfuse `SP3031-01ETG` (DEC-071). GPS1 buzzer FET: `2SK3018W`, LCSC C20069149 (DEC-057). Servo bus: C803 KEMET `T495C686M016ATE250` (68 µF 16 V tantalum, DEC-058) and D807 MDD `SMF7.5A` TVS (DEC-059); 5 V input C102 Kyocera AVX `TPSB476K010R0250` (47 µF 10 V tantalum, DEC-058). Reset diodes D201 and D301: onsemi `BAT54XV2T1G` (DEC-074). The H743↔F100 interface is confirmed as `USART` with the px4io protocol (DEC-015). All other part numbers are `TBD`. This includes regulators, protection devices, power modules, connectors, and interface ICs.

## 1. Project Definition

### 1.1 Purpose and application

A custom PX4 flight controller hardware platform with a dual-MCU architecture. The STM32H743VIT6 is the main flight-controller MCU and drives the 4 tilt servo outputs. The STM32F100C8T6B is the IO MCU: it runs px4io firmware and drives the 4 ESC outputs, the 4 control-surface servo outputs (flaperons, ruddervators), the safety switch and the output failsafe (DEC-015, DEC-016, DEC-050, DEC-051).

Pixhawk 6C and Pixhawk 6X are conceptual and functional references. Their schematic and PCB are not copied. The pin allocation follows Pixhawk 6C (`px4/fmu-v6c`) on the H743 and `px4/io-v2` on the F100, by team decision (DEC-014).

### 1.2 Scope

**In scope:**

- H743 main-MCU hardware architecture
- F100 IO MCU hardware: servo PWM, safety switch, servo rail sense and failsafe
- H743↔F100 USART physical interface
- Sensor interfaces: 2 × SPI IMU, 2 × I2C barometer, 1 × I2C magnetometer (DEC-011)
- H743 external interfaces and F100 PWM/expanded-I/O allocation provided by the user
- 5 V flight-controller board input and 6 V servo supply
- USB FS, BOOT0, SWD, reset, protection, testability, PCB, manufacturing, and bring-up requirements
- Verification planning through hardware bring-up

**Out of scope or deferred:**

- PX4 board port (software team: PX4 v1.16.2, based on `px4/fmu-v6c`, DEC-014)
- Bootloader implementation (PX4 bootloader built from the board config, stock px4io bootloader on the F100, DEC-017, DEC-045) and the HSE bypass changes in the H743 board config and the F100 px4io build (DEC-045)
- Firmware image packaging and software flashing implementation
- Detailed USART message protocol and Software-owned behavior
- Final part numbers outside the part-number boundary above: regulators, protection devices, connectors, power module, and UBEC
- Fabrication release, measured performance, and regulatory approval

### 1.3 Operating scenarios

See section 3.2.

## 2. Requirements Management

### 2.1 Writing rules

- Use **shall** for mandatory requirements, **should** for design preferences, **may** for optional capabilities.
- Each requirement has a stable, unique ID. Never reuse an ID after removal.
- Keep requirements separate from implementation choices, assumptions, and verification results.
- A candidate part, pin mapping, resource count, or implementation report is not verification.
- Deferred firmware details are not hardware approval or release evidence.

### 2.2 Requirement register

All rows are `CONFIRMED` by the user and `NOT_VERIFIED`. SYS-001 and SYS-002 come from the user request.

| ID      | Requirement | Conditions / limits | Verification |
| ------- |---|---|---|
| SYS-001 | The system shall be a custom PX4 Flight Controller hardware platform. | Custom hardware; PX4 implementation details deferred. | Inspection of approved system scope. |
| SYS-002 | Pixhawk 6C (REF-01) and Pixhawk 6X (REF-05) shall be used as conceptual and functional references. | No copied schematic or PCB. The pin allocation follows `px4/fmu-v6c` (H743) and `px4/io-v2` (F100), by team decision (DEC-014). | Architecture review. |
| MCU-001 | The main MCU shall be STM32H743VIT6. | Package placement and native design implementation are open. | Native schematic inspection. |
| MCU-002 | The IO MCU shall be STM32F100C8T6B. | Required during operation. Runs px4io firmware: 8 outputs (4 ESCs and 4 control-surface servos, DEC-050, DEC-051), safety switch, output failsafe (DEC-015, DEC-016). | Native schematic and responsibility review. |
| IF-001  | The H743↔F100 interface shall be USART with the px4io protocol. | H743 USART6 ↔ F100 USART2, 1.5 Mb/s, 3.3 V (DEC-015). The F100 uses this link to detect that the H743 has stopped. Software owns the protocol. | Allocation review and hardware test. |
| SEN-001 | The design shall provide 2 × SPI IMU interfaces. | Part: 2 × ICM-45686 (DEC-008). Resources: pin allocation record (DEC-014). | Schematic inspection and hardware test. |
| SEN-002 | The design shall provide 2 × I2C barometer interfaces. | Parts: ICP-20100 at 0x63 and BMP581 at 0x46, both on I2C2 (DEC-010, DEC-018). Fallback for the BMP581: BMP384 (OPEN-019). | Schematic inspection and hardware test. |
| SEN-003 | The design shall provide 1 × I2C magnetometer interface. | Part: iSentek IST8310 (DEC-011, replaced BMM150 on 2026-10-24). Reduced from 2 by DEC-011. | Architecture and hardware test. |
| PWR-001 | The flight-controller board shall receive 5 V from the power module. | Limits: see PWR-010. | Power-tree inspection and measurement. |
| PWR-002 | The servo supply shall be 6 V. | Limits: see PWR-011. The UBEC plugs into a board-mount XT30; the 8 servo headers share a 6 V bus on the board, Pixhawk style (DEC-019). The board reads the bus (IF-050). | Power-tree inspection and measurement. |
| SAF-001 | If the H743 stops, the F100 shall set its 8 outputs (4 ESCs and 4 control-surface servos, DEC-051) to fixed failsafe values: the ESCs to the disarmed value so the motors stop, the surfaces to a fixed position. No landing is commanded. | Stock px4io acts after 0.5 s without H743 data (PX4 v1.16.2 `px4iofirmware/mixer.cpp`). Failsafe values are PX4 parameters, set after flight tests (decisions file D3). Since DEC-050 the motors stop through the F100 failsafe value on the ESC outputs, not through DShot frame loss (SAF-015). Team, 2026-11-16 (DEC-051): motors off, no descent throttle, no landing; surfaces at 0°, later the glide trim from flight tests; the tilt servos are on the H743 and hold their last angle on an H743 hang (accepted). Changed from "immediate landing" by DEC-016. | Fault-injection test. |

## 3. System Requirements

### 3.1 Functional requirements

| ID      | Required behavior | Condition | Verification |
| ------- |---|---|---|
| FUN-001 | H743 shall provide the primary flight-controller processing and primary system interface resources. | Normal operation | Schematic/resource inspection; later bring-up |
| FUN-002 | H743 shall drive the 4 tilt servo outputs (IF-034 to IF-037, AUX 1 to 4) on TIM5 (PA0 to PA3) with PWM. | Normal operation. Changed by DEC-050 and DEC-051 (was 4 ESC outputs with bidirectional DShot, DEC-016). Servo PWM rate `TBD`. | Resource inspection; hardware test |
| FUN-003 | F100 shall drive the 4 ESC outputs (IF-040 to IF-043, MAIN 1 to 4) with PWM and the 4 control-surface servo outputs (IF-030 to IF-033, MAIN 5 to 8) at 50 Hz PWM, and the safety switch, safety LED and servo rail sense (IF-049, IF-050), and shall read the RC PPM input (IF-038). | Normal operation. RC PPM input on PA8 since DEC-053 (was removed by DEC-018). Output split changed by DEC-050 and DEC-051. ESC throttle 1000 to 2000 µs after calibration; ESC PWM rate `TBD` (team, OPEN-031). | MCU resource review; hardware test |
| FUN-004 | F100 shall hold its 8 outputs (ESCs and control surfaces, DEC-051) at their failsafe values when H743 data stops (see SAF-001). | H743 unavailable | Fault-injection test; timing measurement |
| FUN-005 | H743 and F100 shall provide SWD debug/recovery access. | Development, service, bring-up | Probe connection; debug test |
| FUN-006 | H743 shall retain selectable BOOT0 access. | Development, recovery | Inspection; boot-state test |

### 3.2 Operating scenarios and modes

Exit, transition, recovery, and restart behavior is `TBD` for all rows.

| Scenario / mode   | Entry | Required behavior |
| ----------------- |---|---|
| Normal            | Valid power and normal MCU operation | H743 performs primary functions and drives the tilt servos. F100 drives the ESCs, the flaperons, the ruddervators and the safety switch (DEC-050, DEC-051). |
| H743 unavailable  | No H743 data at the F100 for the SAF-001 timeout | F100 holds the ESCs and control surfaces at their failsafe values: motors off, surfaces at a fixed position (DEC-051). ESC stop time is `NOT_VERIFIED` (OPEN-017). The tilt servos on the H743 hold their last angle on a hang (accepted, DEC-051). |
| H743↔F100 link    | Normal operation | USART with the px4io protocol, 1.5 Mb/s (DEC-015). Software owns the protocol. |
| Boot / service    | BOOT0 or service procedure selects recovery | SWD and USB service access remain available as designed. USB FS on H743 is for later Software-owned flashing/QGC integration. |
| Power input       | Power module connected | Board receives 5 V. Servo supply is 6 V from the UBEC into a board-mount XT30 that feeds the on-board servo bus (DEC-019). Limits are in PWR-010 and PWR-011. |
| USB only          | USB connected, no battery | USB powers the board and all external 5 V ports on the bench (team, REF-09; human owner, IF-022). USB is a bench supply (DEC-038). |
| Power abnormality | Brownout, reset, rail fault, or input loss | Hardware response, sequencing, and safe output state are `TBD`. Verify before release. |

### 3.3 Electrical system requirements

| ID      | Parameter | Min | Nom | Max | Unit | Note |
| ------- |---|---:|---:|---:|---|---|
| PWR-010 | Flight-controller board input | 4.5 | 5 | 5.5 | V | ±10%; source capacity 2 A. Transient limits TBD. Input protection is drawn (D101, OV dividers DEC-036, input capacitors DEC-037); see 4.3. |
| PWR-011 | Servo supply | TBD | 6 | 7.4 | V | Max 7.4 V is the servo rating limit (human owner, 2026-11-19): DS3235 rated 5 to 7.4 V, C507 rated 3.5 to 8.4 V (OPEN-007). Neither sheet gives a separate absolute maximum (`NOT_VERIFIED`), so the UBEC output, its tolerance and transients shall stay at or below 7.4 V. 10 A continuous, 15 A instantaneous, from the user-provided UBEC-10A specification reference. Pulse duration, protection, and distribution TBD. This does not approve a UBEC part. The team's UBEC is a 10 A unit with an XT30 connector (human owner); model TBD. Servo current passes through the board (DEC-019): the XT30 input and the 6 V and GND bus carry the total (OPEN-021). |
| PWR-012 | Board continuous current | TBD | TBD | TBD | A | Separate MCU, sensor, external-I/O, and servo budgets required. |
| PWR-013 | Sensor/MCU rail tolerance | TBD | TBD | TBD | V / % | Diagram rails are candidates, not approved. |
| PWR-014 | Brownout/reset threshold | TBD | TBD | TBD | V | Per MCU and rail. |
| PWR-015 | `+5V` bus in normal operation | TBD | 5 | below 5.5 | V | Human owner, 2026-11-03: "+5V bus must low that 5.5V", for normal operation with both sources inside PWR-010 (chosen over a hard cut at 5.5 V, which the TPS2121 OV threshold cannot give without dropping a healthy 5.2 V input). The bus follows the active input minus the mux drop. The OV trip is a fault guard only (DEC-036). |

Allocations are `CONFIRMED` at the logical and peripheral level (DEC-014, DEC-018). Pins are in the pin allocation record and pinmap review 0.3 (REF-07), not repeated here. For every row, electrical levels, protection and connector pinout are `TBD` and `NOT_VERIFIED` unless noted. Removed rows keep their ID (2.1).

| ID     | Connector | Function | Resource | Note |
| ------ |---|---|---|---|

Telemetry only; RC comes from IF-038 (DEC-053, replaces the ELRS receiver of DEC-018). JST-GH 6-pin, Pixhawk TELEM layout, 5 V supply, TX and RX only, CTS/RTS unconnected (team): the radio's Pixhawk TELEM cable plugs in directly. Radio: 5 V in, 100 mA transmit at 20 dBm, 25 mA receive, 3.3 V UART (Holybro SiK telemetry radio V3 docs page, https://docs.holybro.com/radio/sik-telemetry-radio-v3, `VERIFIED`; 100 mW model `ASSUMED`). |

Its IST8310 is on I2C1; the board IST8310 is on I2C2, so no address clash. Pull-ups TBD. Pinout and the 3.3 V and buzzer pins: OPEN-023. |

| IF-015 | Conn6 / TF-Luna | Rangefinder | UART8 | JST-GH 4-pin (5 V, TX, RX, GND). The team makes an adapter cable from the TF-Luna 6-pin connector (REF-09). |

| IF-018 | Spare output 2 | Spare PWM output | TIM4_CH3 | Output. Was VT L. As IF-017. |
| IF-019 | Spare output 3 | Spare PWM output | TIM4_CH4 | Output. Was VT R. As IF-017. |
| IF-020 | Conn11 / TL2 | Removed | - | TIM15 dropped; its pins carry TIM5_CH3 and TIM5_CH4 (motors 3 and 4 under DEC-016, tilt servos since DEC-051). |

| IF-022 | USB FS | Service, firmware update, QGC | USB FS | Device only; OTG_FS_ID not used. VBUS sense on OTG_FS_VBUS (PA9) through the R603 5k1 / R604 10k divider (DEC-080). USB-C on a board edge, reachable with the canopy open; USB powers the board on the bench without the battery (team, REF-09), including all external 5 V ports (human owner). USB is a bench supply (DEC-038). VBUS and PM02D 5 V must not back-feed each other. ESD: USBLC6-2SC6 drawn (4.2), part not approved (OPEN-010). Routing TBD. |

The 4 ESCs are on MAIN 1 to 4 (TIM2_CH1, TIM2_CH2, TIM4_CH3, TIM4_CH4). First mapping MAIN n = motor n (team, DEC-051); the team sets it in PX4 (QGC Actuators) and may change it. Throttle 1000 to 2000 µs after calibration; failsafe = disarmed value. Weak pull-down (OPEN-018). ESC: one Holybro Tekko32 F4 4in1 50A with AM32 firmware, no PDB (DEC-054; replaces the 4 × Hobbywing Skywalker 80A V2). Its one 8-pin 1.0 mm connector (M1 to M4, GND, VBAT sense up to 25.2 V, current, telemetry; team) reaches MAIN 1 to 4 through a team adapter cable that carries only the 4 signals and GND; VBAT, current and telemetry end unused in the cable. Board connector unchanged: 4 × 3-pin header (signal, +, GND) with the + pin removed, as in the old design (human owner). Tekko32 pinout, BEC and PWM input level `NOT_VERIFIED` (OPEN-034). ESC response to the F100 failsafe pulse: OPEN-017. |

| IF-042 | ESC 3 (front-L) | Motor 3 | F100 MAIN 3, TIM4_CH3 (DEC-051) | As IF-040. |
| IF-043 | ESC 4 (rear-R) | Motor 4 | F100 MAIN 4, TIM4_CH4 (DEC-051) | As IF-040. |
| IF-044 | microSD | Flight logs | SDMMC1, 4-bit | No card-detect pin. |
| IF-045 | H743 debug | Console and SWD | USART3 + SWD | One 6-pin JST-SH in the Pixhawk Debug Mini layout (1 VREF, 2 Console TX, 3 Console RX, 4 SWDIO, 5 SWCLK, 6 GND; PX4 docs; the same order as the DS-009 Pixhawk Debug Mini table, REF-10; the drawn pins were not traced in that check). Reachable after assembly. No NRST pin or pad; reset by B901 (DEC-065). |
| IF-046 | Buzzer | Passive buzzer, PX4 tone alarm | TIM3_CH3 | Same timer and pin as `px4/fmu-v6c`. HRT on TIM8 (no pin). No board buzzer; drives the GPS module buzzer on GPS1 through Q701 (DEC-068). Buzzer pin on GPS1: OPEN-023. |
| IF-047 | Board LEDs | Red, blue, green, active low | 3 × GPIO | H743 PE2 red, PE3 blue, PE4 green on `{LED_MAIN}` (DEC-067). PX4 v1.16.2 `px4/fmu-v6c` drives only red (PD10, `BOARD_OVERLOAD_LED`) and blue (PD11, `BOARD_ARMED_STATE_LED`), active low with anodes to 3.3 V, the same wiring as this board (`board_config.h`, `led.c`; `VERIFIED`). The board config must name PE2 to PE4 and add a green LED entry, or D903 stays off (Software). F100 px4io LEDs: 3.5 (DEC-079). |
| IF-048 | Power module | Battery voltage and current over I2C | I2C4 | Holybro PM02D with TI INA228 at 0x45 (team; older units INA226 at 0x41). Module label not checked (team to send). PC0/PC1 stay reserved as ADC inputs for an analog module (DEC-023). The board connector shall match the PM02D cable. Connector: 6-pin 2.00 mm CLIK-Mate (DEC-042); drawn part in 4.3. |
| IF-052 | ESC current sense | Removed | - | Separate ESCs have no current-sense wire, and the old design had none (human owner, 2026-10-24). The Tekko32 4in1 (DEC-054) has a current wire, but the adapter cable leaves it unused; battery current comes from the PM02D (IF-048). |
| IF-053 | ESC telemetry | Removed | - | As IF-052: the Tekko32 telemetry wire stays unused in the adapter cable (DEC-054). PD0 is free again. |

### 3.5 F100 servo outputs and I/O

`CONFIRMED` by the team (DEC-014, DEC-018). The timer channels are the px4io-v2 outputs (PX4 v1.16.2 `boards/px4/io-v2/src/timer_config.cpp`), default mapping, no AFIO remap. IF-030 to IF-037 are servo PWM at 50 Hz. Since DEC-050 and DEC-051 the 8 F100 outputs carry the 4 ESCs (IF-040 to IF-043, MAIN 1 to 4: TIM2_CH1, TIM2_CH2, TIM4_CH3, TIM4_CH4) and the 4 control surfaces (IF-030 to IF-033, MAIN 5 to 8: TIM3_CH1 to CH4); the tilt servos (IF-034 to IF-037) moved to H743 TIM5 (AUX 1 to 4). No pin changes. Connectors are labelled by output number, MAIN 1 to 8 and AUX 1 to 4; the team sets the actuator mapping in PX4 (QGC Actuators), and the rows below give the first mapping. Electrical output is `TBD`. Servo headers and the on-board 6 V bus: DEC-019; open items: OPEN-021.

| ID     | Logical output | Allocation |
| ------ |---|---|
| IF-030 | Flaperon L | F100 MAIN 5, TIM3_CH1 (DEC-051; was TIM2_CH1) |
| IF-031 | Flaperon R | F100 MAIN 6, TIM3_CH2 (DEC-051; was TIM2_CH2) |
| IF-032 | Ruddervator L | F100 MAIN 7, TIM3_CH3 (DEC-051; was TIM4_CH3) |
| IF-033 | Ruddervator R | F100 MAIN 8, TIM3_CH4 (DEC-051; was TIM4_CH4) |
| IF-034 | Tilt servo 1 (front-R) | H743 AUX 1 to 4, TIM5 on PA0 to PA3 (DEC-051; was F100 TIM3_CH1) |
| IF-035 | Tilt servo 2 (rear-L) | H743 AUX 1 to 4, TIM5 on PA0 to PA3 (DEC-051; was F100 TIM3_CH2) |
| IF-036 | Tilt servo 3 (front-L) | H743 AUX 1 to 4, TIM5 on PA0 to PA3 (DEC-051; was F100 TIM3_CH3) |
| IF-037 | Tilt servo 4 (rear-R) | H743 AUX 1 to 4, TIM5 on PA0 to PA3 (DEC-051; was F100 TIM3_CH4) |
| IF-038 | RC IN (PPM) | F100 PA8, TIM1_CH1, the px4io PPM input (DEC-053; was dropped by DEC-018). FlySky FS-iA6B in PPM mode (team; PX4 has no i-BUS). Connector CN706: 1x3 2.54 mm right-angle header, pin 1 PPM, pin 2 `+5V`, pin 3 `GND`, so the receiver's servo cable plugs in. Signal: D707 SP3031-01ETG ESD at the connector, R715 22 Ω series (R714 before the 16:03 save) into U303 SN74LVC1G34DCKR buffer on `+3V3` (R308 100 kΩ input to `GND`, C311 100 nF), output through R309 22 Ω to PA8, net `RCIN_TIM1_CH1` (DEC-052, DEC-056, DEC-057; the DEC-053 6.2 kΩ / 10 kΩ divider is replaced). Drawn in the 2026-11-19 15:03 to 15:25 saves. Since the 2026-11-20 save CN706 is a 1x3 2.54 mm horizontal header, as in this row. The pin order is pin 1 `+5V`, pin 2 PPM, pin 3 `GND`, kept as drawn (DEC-072; replaces the pin order above) |
| IF-049 | Safety switch and LED | Button GPIO in, LED GPIO out open drain active low (px4io-v2 pins). Reached through GPS1 (IF-012). Disabled in PX4 on Model G; no separate switch (DEC-021) |
| IF-050 | Servo rail sense | ADC1_IN4, 3:1 divider (px4io-v2). Reads the on-board 6 V servo bus (DEC-019) |
| IF-051 | F100 debug | SWD and px4io console (USART1 TX) on test pads TP601 to TP606 (VREF, TX, SWDIO, SWCLK, `RESET_B`, GND), bench use only (DEC-075; was CN603, JST-SH 6-pin in the Debug Mini order, no NRST, DEC-065) |

Other F100 pins that stock px4io-v2 drives or reads (LEDs, RSSI, Spektrum, SBUS, servo fault, board sense) are reserved for that function and not used for anything else (pin allocation record F-13). PA8, the px4io PPM input, is used for RC IN since DEC-053 (IF-038). The three px4io LEDs are fitted (pinmap review Q20, DEC-079): blue on PB14, red on PB15 (px4io `LED_AMBER`; red is fitted in place of amber) and green on PA11, each active low. The green LED is off when armed and breathes when disarmed (DEC-079). PA10 and PB11 stay unconnected with a 10 kΩ pull-up to 3.3 V (pinmap review 0.3, section 4.3; DEC-079).

### 3.6 Environmental and reliability requirements

| ID      | Parameter / condition | Required range or limit | Verification |
| ------- |---|---|---|
| ENV-001 | Operating temperature | Parts rated −40 to +85 °C (DEC-020). Field ambient about 32 °C near Bangkok (team model); the fuselage inside gets hotter in the sun, not measured | Part ratings in the BOM; temperature in the fuselage NOT_VERIFIED |
| ENV-002 | Storage temperature | Within the −40 to +85 °C part ratings (DEC-020); no separate limit given | BOM review |
| ENV-003 | Vibration and shock | No numeric limit for revision 1 (DEC-062). Soft mount on rubber dampers (MEC-002) is the control | PX4 vibration metrics in flight logs (checklist P4-017) |
| ENV-004 | Humidity, ingress, altitude, and maintenance | No rain exposure, no conformal coating, low-altitude flight near sea level (DEC-020). Maintenance: microSD logs read after every flight (MEC-003) | Inspection |

### 3.7 Protection and fault behavior

| ID      | Hazard / fault | Required response | Recovery method | Verification |
| ------- |---|---|---|---|
| SAF-010 | H743 unavailable | See SAF-001. The H743 owns the 4 tilt servo outputs; the F100 owns the 4 ESC and 4 control-surface outputs (DEC-016, DEC-050, DEC-051). | Reset, recovery and F100-failure behavior are Software-owned. The F100 starts its own IWDG (px4io, team answer S1). | Fault-injection test; NOT_VERIFIED |
| SAF-011 | H743↔F100 USART loss | Same response as SAF-010; the F100 cannot tell a link loss from an H743 stop (`ASSUMED`). | As SAF-010. | Fault-injection test; NOT_VERIFIED |
| SAF-012 | 5 V input abnormality | Safe reset/output state TBD. | Power recovery procedure TBD. | Measurement and fault test; NOT_VERIFIED |
| SAF-013 | 6 V servo rail fault | Protection and safe actuator state TBD. The 6 V servo bus is on the board (DEC-019); the F100 reads it (IF-050). | TBD | Measurement and fault test; NOT_VERIFIED |
| SAF-014 | ESD, reverse polarity, overvoltage, short circuit | Protection requirements TBD. | Service/recovery method TBD. | Analysis / test; NOT_VERIFIED |
| SAF-015 | H743 hang with motors running | Motors stop. Since DEC-050 the ESCs are on the F100: 0.5 s after H743 data stops, the F100 sets the ESC outputs to their failsafe value (`PWM_MAIN_DISn` by default, DEC-016), which shall stop the motors. PX4 v1.16.2 does not start the H743 IWDG (team answer S1, not checked by hardware). Depends on the ESC's response to that pulse (OPEN-017). | H743 reset or power cycle. | Bench test: halt the H743 over SWD while armed; NOT_VERIFIED |

## 4. Hardware Architecture and Design Constraints

### 4.1 Functional block diagram

The user-provided diagrams show an H743 main MCU, an F100 auxiliary MCU, sensor/interface blocks, USB/SWD, PWM/actuator paths, and a separate power architecture. The images are reference inputs only. Native schematic and PCB files remain the design authority.

### 4.2 Functional blocks

| Block                     | Role | Inputs / outputs | Dependencies | Key constraints |
| ------------------------- |---|---|---|---|
| H743 main controller      | Primary flight-controller processing and interface host | Sensors, external interfaces, USB, SWD, USART, outputs | 5 V input and defined rails | Pins/resources NOT_VERIFIED. |
| F100 IO controller        | px4io: ESC and control-surface PWM (DEC-050, DEC-051), safety switch, output failsafe | USART link, 8 PWM outputs, safety switch and LED, servo rail sense | H743 data; defined power/reset; 24 MHz CMOS oscillator (DEC-017, DEC-044) | Electrical implementation NOT_VERIFIED. |
| IMU subsystem             | Two SPI IMU interfaces | SPI, CS, DRDY; reset by software (DEC-009) | Selected sensor parts and rail | 2 × ICM-45686 (DEC-008). Rail: VDD on `+3V3A`, VDDIO on `+3V3` (DEC-043). |
| Barometer subsystem       | Two I2C barometer interfaces | I2C, address, pull-ups | Selected sensor parts and rail | ICP-20100 + BMP581 (DEC-010), both on I2C2 with the IST8310. |
| Magnetometer subsystem    | One I2C magnetometer interface | I2C, address, placement | Selected sensor part | IST8310 (DEC-011). Placement TBD. |
| H743 external I/O         | Telemetry/RC, GPS, compass, air-data, rangefinder, power module, 4 tilt servo outputs (DEC-051), 3 spare outputs, microSD, buzzer, LEDs | UART/I2C/timer/SDMMC interfaces | H743 resource allocation | Electrical implementation NOT_VERIFIED. |
| USB/SWD/BOOT0             | Service, debug, recovery | USB FS, debug connector (IF-045), F100 SWD pads (IF-051), BOOT0 | H743/F100 reset and power | Protection and circuit NOT_VERIFIED. |
| Power subsystem           | 5 V board input and 6 V servo supply | Rails, returns, protection | Power module and distribution | Tolerances and current TBD. Input protection drawn (4.3). |

Findings: MAJOR, GPS1 buzzer pin driven straight from H743 PB0 (OPEN-037); MAJOR, no I2C4 pull-ups fitted (OPEN-038); MAJOR, C803 10 V solid tantalum at 60 to 74 % of its rating on the hot-plugged servo bus (OPEN-021); MINOR, D807 standoff below 7.4 V (OPEN-021); MINOR, `GPS1_SAFETY_LED` is active low without `_B` (OPEN-032); MINOR, PB5 safety switch input has no board pull (OPEN-037); MINOR, `+3V3` exported on CN705 pin 8 (OPEN-037); INFO, PA4 injection about 26 µA at a 12 V TVS clamp (`ESTIMATED`), within the ±5 mA absolute maximum (DocID16455 Table 6) but outside the PA4 functional susceptibility (Table 33, 0 mA); INFO, about 67 µF of on-board `+6V` bulk against 13.2 A stall (OPEN-021); INFO, spare outputs with no pads (OPEN-032). Not checked: DS-009 pinouts, ESD, TVS, header and XT30 ratings (no notebook); capacitor ratings come from the `Rate` fields, read 2026-11-19 after the review, and the bus links `{ESC}`, `{AUX_TIM5}`, `{SURFACE}`, `{EXT_I2C4}` (not traced by `sch-inspect`; ERC clean). A targeted review, not a PASS. Each member reaches the pin the pin allocation gives it, under one net name (`VERIFIED`, KiCad netlist). No connectivity defect found within the inspected scope. Reported as checked by the subagent (`ASSUMED` here unless re-checked): sensor decoupling against each datasheet, I2C addresses 0x63, 0x46 and 0x0C, IST8310 VPP, ICM-45686 unused pins, ICP-20100 CSB and RESV, no USB D+ or D- series resistors needed (DS12110 Table 102), CC1 and CC2 5.1 kΩ, CN602 in the IF-045 order. Re-checked by Claude (`VERIFIED`, cited): IST8310 RSTN has an internal pull-up (the subagent's MAJOR is withdrawn); ICM-45686 pin 7 on `GND` needs firmware to turn off its pull-up (DS-000489 Table 10); BMP581 INT unused should go to `GND` (§6.2); ICP-20100 INT is open-drain with an internal pull-up (DS-000416 §8). Findings closed by DEC-081 to DEC-085. INFO: CN601 shield goes straight to `GND` (U601 needs a short ground path at layout); IST8310 re-POR needs AVDD below 0.1 V for 10 ms, so the driver should soft-reset; at 400 kHz the 5.1 kΩ I2C2 pull-ups allow about 69 pF (`ESTIMATED`). Not checked: the U601 pass-through pinout on the ST datasheet (ST page timed out), the `+3V3` ramp against the ICM-45686 limit, power-down order. A targeted review, not a PASS.

### 4.3 Power architecture

- Board input and servo supply limits: see PWR-010 and PWR-011.
- The diagram-derived arrangement is a candidate only: 6S battery, XT90-S, PM02D, 5 V, 3.3 V, 3.3 V_IMU, 6 V UBEC.
- No PDB (DEC-054; human owner, 2026-11-19): the PM02D battery output feeds the Tekko32 4in1 ESC battery pads, and the 6 V UBEC is soldered to the same pads. All motor and servo current passes through the PM02D; its current rating against that load is `NOT_VERIFIED`.
- The 6 V UBEC plugs into a board-mount XT30. The 8 headers share an on-board 6 V and servo GND bus, so all servo current passes through the board (DEC-019, OPEN-021). Drawn as CN804 Amass XT30PW-M on `conn-actuator` (2026-11-19 11:38 save, 4.2).
- USB VBUS powers the board on the bench without the battery (IF-022). It reaches `+5V` through the power mux IN2 (DEC-029). USB is a bench supply (DEC-038). The PM02D has priority, with the XCOMP fast switchover (DEC-031 to DEC-034).
- 5 V input: TI TPS2121 power mux (DEC-029, 2026-11-01, replaces the DEC-026 eFuse): IN1 from the PM02D (`PM02_5V`), IN2 from USB `VBUS`, OUT to the `+5V` bus, current limit 1.5 A (DEC-030). TI data for the drawn parts is in CMP-008 and 4.7. The TPS2121 blocks reverse current on both channels (CMP-008), so neither source back-feeds the other through it.
- Main 3.3 V rail: TPS62142RGTR buck from the 5 V board input (DEC-012).
- Sensor rail: `+3V3A` feeds the VDD pins of the 2 IMUs, both barometers and the magnetometer (IST8310 AVDD); their VDDIO pins (IST8310 DVDD) stay on `+3V3` (DEC-043, replaces DEC-025). From a TPS73533DRBR LDO (DEC-027).
- TBD: rail dependencies, current budgets, load steps, sequencing, brownout, reverse-polarity protection, grounding, filtering, thermal limits. Input overvoltage and transient parts are drawn (D101, DEC-036, DEC-037; see the table below).

The table uses the references of the re-annotated sheet (commit `4d55a7e` and later saves); a reference in spec revisions before 0.29 may name a different part. The 2026-11-14 read found only the hierarchical labels `PM02_SCL` and `PM02_SDA`; in the 2026-11-17 15:41 save they are one bus label `{EXT_I2C4}` with `EXT_I2C4_SCL` and `EXT_I2C4_SDA` (DEC-052); `3V3_PG` is now a local net (4.2 "Schematic state"). The human owner declared the sheet's capture complete on 2026-10-31; later changes are recorded in the DEC rows from DEC-029. This records what is drawn. It is not a reviewed or approved design. 53 parts, all annotated (U101 to U103, C101 to C125, R101 to R115, L101, L102, D101 to D106, Q101, CN101); D105 and D106 are the parked DNP diodes. Resistor MFR, MFR.P/N and LCSC fields are empty; the human owner chooses them later (P2-021). Five `PWR_FLAG` symbols: on `PM02_5V`, `VBUS`, `+5V_BUCK`, `GND` and `+3V3`. `VBUS` is now driven by the USB-C receptacle CN601 on `conn-usb-debug` (4.2), so the `VBUS` flag is temporary (P2-022: remove it if ERC allows). ERC run by the human owner on 2026-11-03: only the unconnected hierarchical sheet pins were left (then `3V3_PG`, `PM02_SCL` and `PM02_SDA`), set aside until the other sheets were drawn (ERC report not seen by Claude). ERC does not show the circuit is correct. The project ERC pin map treats Output to Output and Power output to Power output as no error (DEC-041). This clears the stacked same-net pins (U101 OUT pins 1 and 8, U102 SW pins 1 to 3), but it also hides two real outputs or two regulators driving one net on any other sheet (MINOR, potential risk, `VERIFIED` from the file). Decided: the power mux and its settings (DEC-029 to DEC-034, DEC-036), the status LED (DEC-035), the input capacitors (DEC-037), the LDO enable (DEC-039), the buck (DEC-012), the LDO (DEC-027), the 10 nF SS/TR value (DEC-028) and the PM02D connector type (DEC-042). ΔI_L is about 0.2 A at 5.5 V in, so 3.5 A covers the full 2 A rating with the 20 % margin (2.5 A needed) and matches the 3.5 A static current limit maximum (`ESTIMATED`). Output 30 µF nominal sits between the proven 22 µF and 47 µF cells (`ESTIMATED`; DC-bias derating at 3.3 V not checked). Input filter: L101 0.33 µH with C111 to C113 (about 18 µF effective, `ASSUMED`) resonates near 65 kHz with Z0 about 0.14 Ω. L101 DCR 130 to 170 mΩ (distributor listings, `NOT_VERIFIED`; the Note field "330nH 170mR" and the sheet's filter note use 170 mΩ: Q 0.8, Z_MAX about 0.22 Ω, 20 dB below a 2.2 Ω input impedance) gives Q about 1, and Z0 is far below the buck input impedance (2.6 Ω at 2 A out), so it is damped (`ESTIMATED`). L101 is rated 1.2 A (distributor listings, `NOT_VERIFIED`): enough for the expected buck input of about 0.5 A (power budget section 4), not for the about 1.7 A input at the full 2 A output. L101 stays for the 0.6 A `+3V3` design current (DEC-040). | Netlist export, 2026-11-01; TI SLVSAJ0F Rev. F, Table 6-1, 8.3, 9.2.2.2 and Tables 9-2 and 9-3 (NotebookLM source fulltext) |

**Schematic review (`sch-review` by Claude, 2026-11-03).** Pin nets of U101, U102, U103, Q101, CN101, D101 to D104, L101 and L102 traced with `sch-inspect`; no IC pin floating. No defect found within the inspected scope. L101 kept (DEC-040); ERC pin map change kept (DEC-041). Open: ST low level against the Q101 threshold (P4-012, OPEN-025). D101 breakdown voltage 6.4 V checked by the human owner ("D101 fine, 6.4V Br i checked all", 2026-11-03; Claude did not read the datasheet). INFO: the `PWR_FLAG` symbols on the inductor-fed `+5V_BUCK` and `+3V3` would also hide a missing source after a later edit; `3V3_PG` drives the LDO EN through a 1 ms RC, so an MCU pin added to it must stay an input. Fault cases checked (`ESTIMATED`): source switchover, input overvoltage, hot plug, unpowered SPI interface at power-up, `+5V` load, `+3V3` short (buck limit 2.45 to 3.5 A, L102 saturation 3.5 A, PG low turns the LDO off). Not verified: L101 and L102 DCR, DC-bias derating of the 10 µF parts, TPS2121 behavior after current limit, TPS735 EN hysteresis, LED currents, reverse polarity on CN101 (keyed connector; TPS2121 negative input rating not checked), PCB. This is a targeted review of the drawn sheet, not an approval.

**Power mux library part (project library, 2026-11-01).** Symbol `0_Power_Management:TPS2121RUX`, drawn from the SLVSEA3F pin table (12 pins, pins match the footprint pads). Footprint `0_Package_DFN_QFN:Texas_VQFN-HR-12_2x2.5mm_P0.5mm_ThermalVias`, a stock copy whose pads match the RUX0012A land pattern (8 pads 0.2 × 0.6 mm and 4 pads 1.05 × 0.4 mm at the TI positions, `VERIFIED`). 3D model `Texas_VQFN-HR-12_2x2.5mm_P0.5mm_TPS2121RUXR.step`, supplied by the human owner. Pin 1 corner against the TI drawing and the 3D model alignment are human checks. A second, unused stock copy without thermal vias is also in the library. In the working copy (read 2026-11-14), the library symbol and U101 on the sheet point to that copy, `Texas_VQFN-HR-12_2x2.5mm_P0.5mm`, instead of the `_ThermalVias` one (drawn, not decided).

**Q101 library part (project library, 2026-11-03, not committed).** Symbol `0_Transistor_FET:2SK3018W` (pins 1 G, 2 S, 3 D) with footprint `0_Package_TO_SOT_SMD:SOT-323_SC-70` (3 pads). The human owner checked the pin map against the 2SK3018W datasheet (2026-11-03, "i checked that pin"); Claude did not read the datasheet. The earlier `0_Transistor_FET:DMG1012UW` symbol (committed in `6c2526d`) is no longer placed. The `2N7002K` symbol changed only the position of its hidden Description field.

**Input tantalum footprint (project library, 2026-11-03, not committed).** `0_Capacitor_SMD:CP_EIA-3528-21_Kemet-B` is a copy of the stock KiCad footprint: same pads (pad 1 is +, 1.34 × 2.39 mm at ±1.54 mm) and the stock 3D model path; only the fab outline, the reference text and the hidden Datasheet and Description fields differ (diff against the stock file, `VERIFIED`).

The root, `top_hierarchical`, `block_diagram` and `revision_history` sheets hold no parts; `top_hierarchical` has the power sheet symbol with the sheet pin `{EXT_I2C4}` (was `PM02_SCL` and `PM02_SDA`), which reaches H743 I2C4 (4.2 "Schematic state"). The root sheet carries a "DESIGN CONVENTIONS" text note (style, reference letters, units, part fields) and, since the 2026-11-17 15:41 save, a second note with the DEC-052 net naming rules (it replaces the old NET NAMING block; both notes are titled "DESIGN CONVENTIONS", and the part-field line still reads `MPR.P/N`). Since 2026-11-03 the title blocks of every sheet and the PCB show revision `A`, and the project text variables set `PROJECT_ID` `NP-AO-2601`, `SCH_DRAWING_NAME`, `SCH_DRAWING_DATE` 01-Nov-2026, `RELEASE_DATE` 02-Nov-2026 and `STATUS` `DRAFT`, shown on the root sheet (since commit `f594e29`: `SCH_DRAWING_DATE` 11-Nov-2026, `RELEASE_DATE` Dec-2026). These are drawing fields; they do not set the hardware phase row above. The saved PCB has no footprints. Its `Edge.Cuts` rectangle is 80 × 80 mm (110,60 to 190,140; commit `f594e29`, was 27 × 29 mm): a working area, not the board outline (human owner, 2026-11-19); the outline is open in OPEN-009.

### 4.4 Clock, reset, boot and programming

- H743 shall retain selectable BOOT0 access (FUN-006). Drawn as push button B902 to `+3V3` with R202 to `GND` (DEC-095); boot-state test in P4.
- H743 and F100 shall provide SWD access (FUN-005).
- USB FS shall be physically available on H743 for later Software-owned flashing/QGC integration.
- H743 clock: 16 MHz HSE, as `px4/fmu-v6c` (DEC-017), from a CMOS oscillator into PH0 (DEC-044), with the HSE in bypass mode (`STM32_HSEBYP_ENABLE` in the board config, DEC-045). No LSE.
- F100 clock: 24 MHz HSE from a CMOS oscillator into PD0 (OSC_IN) (DEC-044), used directly as SYSCLK, as in stock px4io-v2, with the HSE in bypass mode set by a custom px4io build (DEC-017, DEC-045; PX4 v1.16.2 `boards/px4/io-v2/nuttx-config/include/board.h`). The F100 HSE crystal range is 4 to 24 MHz (DocID16455 Rev 9 Table 21, OPEN-020); U302 meets the external-clock (bypass) limits of DocID16455 Rev 9 Table 19 (CMP-010; margins in OPEN-028). The stock px4io bootloader runs from HSI. No LSE; PC14 and PC15 unconnected (px4io reads them at boot).
- F100 boot: BOOT0 pulled down (R307 10 kΩ); BOOT1 (PB2) has no resistor, because it is ignored while BOOT0 = 0 (DEC-095). The software team flashes the px4io bootloader over SWD once; after that PX4 updates the F100 over the USART link. No BOOT0/NRST control from the H743.
- H743 first flash: PX4 bootloader over SWD (software team, ST-LINK); updates over USB.
- Boot-time outputs: every PWM line low before firmware starts, with a weak pull-down and no pull-up (team, pinmap review Q10). Value 100 kΩ (DEC-073). Applies to all 15 PWM lines: MAIN 1 to 8, AUX 1 to 4, SPARE 1 to 3 (DEC-069). 100 kΩ to `GND` on all 15 lines as drawn in the 2026-11-20 save (4.2 actuator and peripheral connectors rows), decided 2026-11-20 (DEC-073).
- Watchdogs: px4io starts the F100 IWDG; PX4 v1.16.2 does not start the H743 IWDG (team answer S1).
- Reset: one NRST net shared by the H743 and the F100, with one reset button (DEC-046).
- TBD: the reset button part and whether an internal MCU reset drives NRST (OPEN-026), power-on reset, service procedures.

### 4.5 Control, sensing, analog and digital considerations

All items are `TBD` and `NOT_VERIFIED`.

- SPI/I2C: bus ownership, chip-select, data-ready/interrupt, pull-up, address, DMA, timing, recovery.
- PWM: output voltage, drive method, current capability, actuator connector pinout. Since DEC-050 no line carries DShot; all outputs are PWM (OPEN-018).
- USART: protection and test access. Rate (1.5 Mb/s) and protocol (px4io) are set by DEC-015.
- Signal integrity, return paths, sensor noise, magnetic placement, EMI/EMC constraints.

### 4.7 Engineering calculations and margins

| Analysis               | Inputs / operating corner | Method / reference | Result | Margin / open questions |
| ---------------------- |---|---|---|---|
| Board input current    | 5 V input, MCU/sensor/I/O load | TBD | NOT_VERIFIED | Current budget and duty cycle TBD |
| 5 V power mux settings (U101) | RILM 80.6 kΩ (DEC-030), PR1 divider 12.1 kΩ / 5.1 kΩ from IN1 (DEC-031, DEC-034), CP2 divider 16.5 kΩ (fitted, DEC-033) / 5.1 kΩ from `VBUS` (DEC-032), OV dividers 23.2 kΩ / 5.1 kΩ on both inputs (DEC-036; bottom resistors 5.1 kΩ since DEC-055), ST divider 10 kΩ / 10 kΩ from `+5V` (DEC-036), CSS 10 nF; `+5V` and `+5V_BUCK` capacitance as drawn, about 60 µF nominal | TI SLVSEA3F Electrical Characteristics, 9.3.1, 9.3.2, 9.3.7, Table 9-1 and 10.2.4.2 (CMP-008) | Limit 1.0 / 1.5 / 2.0 A from the 80 kΩ table row (`VERIFIED`); 1.49 A typ from `ILM = 65.2 / RILM^0.861`, RILM in kΩ (`ESTIMATED`: equation form read from the flattened text, it reproduces TI's 59 kΩ to 1.95 A example). OV trip 5.88 V typ, 5.61 to 6.10 V over the VREF rising range, 5.51 to 6.21 V with 1 % resistors (`ESTIMATED`, TI equation 6 form); the channel turns back on below 5.77 V typ (VREF falling, `ESTIMATED`). The lowest trip, 5.51 V, is 14 mV above the 5.5 V input maximum (PWR-010, PWR-015). With the 5 kΩ bottom resistors before DEC-055: 5.98 V typ, 5.60 to 6.31 V, back on below 5.87 V. ST divider: ST and the Q101 gate at 2.23 V for `+5V` 4.5 V and 3.13 V for 6.21 V with 1 % resistors, below the ST 5.5 V recommended and the 6 V ST absolute maximum; ST sinks about 0.55 mA through R109 when low (`ESTIMATED`). Soft-start: TI Table 9-1 starts at 100 nF (780 V/s at 5 V); scaled as 1/C, 10 nF gives about 7.8 kV/s, about 0.65 ms to 5 V and about 0.47 A inrush into 60 µF (`ESTIMATED`, outside the table range). PR1 ratio 3.37, CP2 ratio 4.24 (TI equation 5 and 12 form, `ESTIMATED`, before resistor tolerance). XCOMP (R105 fitted): CP2 1.12 / 1.18 / 1.24 V for `VBUS` 4.75 / 5.0 / 5.25 V; switch to USB when the PM02D falls to 3.78 / 3.98 / 4.18 V (switch point `= CP2 × 17.2 / 5.1`; unchanged from 5 kΩ within 0.01 V). Worst case at `VBUS` 5.25 V: 1 % resistors in the bad direction give CP2 1.26 V and a PR1 ratio of 0.2924, so 4.31 V; the 40 mV maximum comparator offset (TI 7.5, 5 / 20 / 40 mV, `VERIFIED`) adds 0.14 V, so 4.44 V, 0.06 V below the 4.5 V input minimum (PWR-010); the 12.4 kΩ case is in DEC-034. TI Table 9-3 (`VERIFIED`): IN1 needs PR1 ≥ VREF and PR1 > CP2, so the VREF-mode point below is the lowest IN1 the mux keeps. CP2 reaches the 1.10 V VREF maximum at `VBUS` 4.66 V (4.73 V with 1 % resistors); below that a part may run in the VREF mode (same priority, 100 µs). VREF mode (R105 not fitted): to USB at 3.34 / 3.51 / 3.68 V, back at 3.41 / 3.58 / 3.71 V. PR1 at the 6.21 V OV trip maximum: 1.84 V, below its 5.5 V limit. Divider currents about 0.29 mA (PR1) and 0.23 mA (CP2) at 5 V. Switchover dip in the VREF mode: up to 100 µs, `VDIP = IOUT × tSW / COUT`, about 1.7 V at 1 A into 60 µF (`ESTIMATED`, nominal capacitance) | Minimum limit 1.0 A against the 5 V load estimate (OPEN-006). XCOMP switch point 4.44 V worst case with 1 % resistors, 0.06 V below the 4.5 V minimum (DEC-034). OV trip 5.51 V minimum, 14 mV above the 5.5 V input maximum (DEC-036, DEC-055; accepted, OPEN-036 closed). Re-run 2026-11-19 for DEC-055 with the same method and the CMP-008 VREF values; the 5 kΩ inputs reproduce the earlier results. Use 1 % resistors in the PR1, CP2, OV and ST dividers |
| Buck soft-start (U102) | SS/TR 10 nF (DEC-028), ISS 2.5 µA typ (2.3 to 2.7 µA), internal reference 1.25 V (equation 10 constant, as extracted) | TI SLVSAJ0F equation 10, ISS from Electrical Characteristics | 5 ms typ, about 4.6 to 5.4 ms over the ISS range, before capacitor tolerance (`ESTIMATED`) | Capacitor C115; part fields in 4.3 (Part fields row) |
| Servo rail current     | 6 V rail and actuator load (on the board, DEC-019) | Sum of the 6 V datasheet stall currents: 4 × 2.1 A (DS3235) + 4 × 1.2 A (C507) (OPEN-007) | 13.2 A with all 8 stalled (`ESTIMATED`) | 8 servos can move at once (team). Tilt servos 4 × RDS3235; surfaces 4 × Kingmax C507 V2. 32 % over the 10 A UBEC continuous rating, under the 15 A peak. Running current under load TBD; team bench target: 4 tilt servos at or below 10 A. Sizes the UBEC (OPEN-007) and the board input and bus (OPEN-021) |
| Power rail tolerance   | All MCU/sensor rails | TBD | NOT_VERIFIED | Nominal, min, max, and load-step limits TBD |
| Thermal margin         | MCU, regulator, protection, output drivers | TBD | NOT_VERIFIED | Ambient, dissipation, copper, and cooling TBD |
| Safety response timing | H743 stop to servo failsafe; H743 hang to motor stop | px4io `FMU_INPUT_DROP_LIMIT_US` = 500000 (PX4 v1.16.2 `px4iofirmware/mixer.cpp`) | Servos: 0.5 s by firmware design (VERIFIED in source, not measured). Motors: NOT_VERIFIED | Motor stop time depends on the 0.5 s F100 timeout and the ESC response to the failsafe pulse (DEC-050, OPEN-017). Test: halt the H743 over SWD |

### 5.1 Mechanical envelope

| ID      | Item | Requirement / limit | Verification |

| MEC-001 | Board outline | Each board dimension shall be less than `80 mm × 80 mm`. Exact outline and shape TBD. This supersedes the older diagram size of about `90 mm × 95 mm`. | Native PCB inspection / measurement |
| MEC-002 | Mounting | M3 screws, 3.2 mm hole with pad (human owner); the CAD team follows the hardware. Soft mount on rubber dampers; the dampers are a separate part and do not change the PCB. The board sits on its own tray on the fuselage floor near the CG, not on the ESC (human owner). Damper model: hardware picks it at placement | Native PCB inspection / measurement |
| MEC-003 | Connector orientation and service access | Any edge may face forward (PX4 sets the board rotation); the forward direction shall be marked on the silkscreen. USB-C, microSD and the H743 debug connector shall be reachable with the canopy open (team, REF-09). USB-C and microSD on adjacent edges (human owner). Cable entry sides TBD, set by hardware | Mechanical inspection |
| MEC-004 | Height, keepouts, enclosure, environmental constraints | Part height about 10 to 15 mm, with the Pixhawk 6C as the reference; hardware sets the final value at placement (human owner). The GPS sits above the board under the canopy; the CAD team draws the space to fit the board (OPEN-009) | Mechanical inspection / analysis |

| DEC-037 | `PM02_5V` input capacitors (U101 IN1, PWR-010, PWR-015) | Two 10 µF X7R 16 V 0805 (GRM21BZ71C106KE15L) and one 47 µF 10 V MnO2 tantalum with 0.25 Ω ESR (Kyocera AVX TPSB476K010R0250, case B), fitted, next to C101, C102 (now C105) and D101 | Added by the human owner ("i added 2x10uF and 0.25DCR 47uF 10V tantalum on PM02_5V, check it") after TI SLVSEA3F section 11 warned of cable ringing with long input cables, with the OV trip as low as 5.60 V (DEC-036). Hot-plug step simulation (5.2 V, 0.5 to 2 µH cable, about 6 µF real capacitance per 10 µF, no TVS): peak 5.8 to 6.4 V with the tantalum, 8.7 to 9.5 V with the ceramics alone, about 10.3 V with 2 × 100 nF (`ESTIMATED`). The 10 V rating runs at 55 % at 5.5 V and about 60 % at the simulated peak, above the common 50 % derating guidance for MnO2 tantalum in low-impedance circuits (`ASSUMED`; the AVX derating paper was not read). AVX lists the TPS series as 100 % surge current tested (AVX TPS series page, `VERIFIED`). Human owner: "record 10V, it's fine" (2026-11-03). Bench check: P4-013. | 2026-11-03 |

| DEC-039 | `+3V3A` LDO enable and power-up order (U103, OPEN-011, DEC-025) | U103 EN from the buck power-good `3V3_PG` (through R115 0 Ω, with C120 10 nF to `GND`), not from `+5V`, so `+3V3A` starts after `+3V3` | Human owner chose option 2: "2, i connected LDO EN to 3V3_PG + RC filter, check it", then "i changed and use 0R" (2026-11-03). Reason: with EN on `+5V`, `+3V3A` came up about 5 ms before `+3V3`, and the ICM-45686 SPI pull-ups (enabled by default, DS-000489 Table 10, `VERIFIED`) held the H743 TT pins PA4, PA5 and PA7 at about 3.3 V while the H743 was unpowered: inside the 4.0 V absolute maximum (DS12110 Table 109, `VERIFIED`), outside the VDD + 0.3 V operating limit (Table 112). With EN from PG, the H743 pins are floating inputs after reset while the IMUs are off (DS12110, `VERIFIED`), and the LDO starts from EN in about 50 µs typ (TI SBVS087M 6.5, `VERIFIED`), inside the IMU's 0.01 to 1 ms supply ramp for a valid power-on reset (DS-000489 Table 4, `VERIFIED`; ramp `ESTIMATED`). Steady-state SPI levels match with both rails at 3.3 V (H743 VOH ≥ VDD − 0.4 V, IMU VIH ≥ 0.7 × VDDIO; IMU VOH ≥ 0.9 × VDDIO, H743 VIH ≥ 0.7 × VDD; `VERIFIED` limits). Since DEC-043 the IMU VDDIO is on `+3V3`. EN threshold hysteresis is `NOT_VERIFIED`. Bench check: P4-015. | 2026-11-03 |

Evidence, PX4-Autopilot `v1.16.2` and its NuttX submodule `886acbb` (`VERIFIED` from source): `fmu-v6c` `board.h` does not define `STM32_HSEBYP_ENABLE`, and NuttX `stm32h7x3xx_rcc.c` sets HSEBYP only when it is defined (lines 686 to 693). The `fmu-v6c` bootloader defconfig uses the same `nuttx-config` folder (line 14) and ships prebuilt in `extras/`, so a stock H743 bootloader would also start the HSE in crystal mode. `io-v2` runs the HSE as SYSCLK (`board.h` lines 57 to 65); NuttX `stm32f10xxx_rcc.c` `stm32_stdclockconfig()` clears HSEBYP with no board option (lines 628 to 645), and the `io-v2` defconfig has no custom clock config. | DEC-052 | Net label naming (OPEN-032, P2-022); amended 2026-11-17 to be flexible; MCU prefix rule amended by DEC-064 | Fixed rules: (1) Format: UPPERCASE, A-Z 0-9 and `_` only, full words (`SAFETY`, not `SAFT`), one name per net. (2) A signal net that leaves its sheet starts with the port it serves (`GPS1_`, `TEL1_`, `IMU1_`, `MAIN1_`, `DBG_`); on an MCU peripheral pin it also names the peripheral, `<PORT>_<PERIPH>_<SIGNAL>` (`GPS1_UART2_TX`, `NAV_I2C2_SCL`); a plain GPIO may drop the peripheral (`LED_RED`, `SAFETY_SW`). (3) `_B` marks active-low; `_P`/`_N` only for differential pairs. (4) A sheet pin has the same name as its hierarchical label, and a bus member has the same name as the label on the pin. Free choice of the designer: the bus name (`{PORT}` or `{PORT}_{PERIPH}`, for example `{GPS1}`, `{AUX_TIM5}`, `{SPARE_TIM}`, `{ESC}`); the port numbering (`MAINn`, `AUXn`, `SPAREn`), which follows the connector labels; the `H7_` or `F1_` prefix on nets that belong to one MCU, which is required on nets local to an MCU sheet (`H7_VDDA`, `H7_BOOT0`, stubs, the MCU side of a series resistor) and allowed elsewhere (`H7_SWDIO`, `H7_SWD_VREF33`); and the names of nets local to a non-MCU sheet, within rule (1). Names drawn in the 16:40 save are examples, not a fixed list; the schematic holds the exact names and the pin allocation maps them to pins | Human owner: "use your convention, record it as a DEC row" (2026-11-17), then "change the decision, make it flexible" (2026-11-17). The first version listed every name with its pin and fixed the bus names and spare numbering; this version keeps the rules and leaves those to the designer. Spare numbering drawn in the 16:40 save (`SPARE1` = TIM1_CH1 on PE9, `SPARE2` = TIM4_CH3, `SPARE3` = TIM4_CH4) differs from the order of IF-017 to IF-019 in the pin allocation; the pin allocation follows once the connector labels are set (OPEN-032). The root-sheet NET NAMING note shows the first version (`{PORT}` buses, fixed port list) and should be updated to these rules. Whether the KiCad diff-pair router detects the USB pair names is `NOT_VERIFIED`. | 2026-11-17 |

| DEC-079 | F100 px4io LEDs and input pull-ups (3.5; pinmap review Q20 and 0.3; IF-047) | As drawn in the 2026-11-20 13:37 save: `+3V3` through 220 Ω (C112291) to each LED anode, cathode on the F100 pin, active low: D905 blue LTST-C191TBKT (R905) on PB14 `LED_IO_BLUE_B`, D904 red LTST-C191KRKT (R904) on PB15 `LED_IO_RED_B` (px4io `LED_AMBER`; red in place of amber) and D906 green 19-21/GHC-YR1S2/4T (R906) on PA11 `LED_IO_GREEN_B`, on `{LED_IO}` to `user-interface`. R312 and R311 10 kΩ (C144807) from PA10 and PB11 to `+3V3` | Human owner (2026-11-20): "Add LEDs and pull-ups"; green "Active low (like others)"; amber "Fit it"; "i will use red led replace by amber". PX4 v1.16.2 `boards/px4/io-v2/src/board_config.h` (`VERIFIED`): all four px4io LED pins are open drain (lines 73 to 76); `LED_BLUE`, `LED_AMBER` and `LED_SAFETY` write `!(on_true)` (active low), `LED_GREEN` writes `(on_true)` (line 99), so with active-low wiring the green LED is off when armed (`px4io.cpp` line 186) and breathes when disarmed (line 207, "currently inverted"). PC14 and PC15 are unconnected, so the board sense reads `SENSE_PH1` and PB14 drives the blue LED, not the Pixhawk 2.1 heater (lines 89 to 104; netlist). PB11 is a floating SBUS input (line 151) and PA10 can be a push-pull Spektrum bind output (lines 107, 120); a 10 kΩ pull-up suits both. The amber (red) LED lights only when the H743 writes the px4io test-LED register (`registers.c`, `ASSUMED`, read by a subagent). LED current and F100 sink current `NOT_VERIFIED`; brightness on the bench, as DEC-067. | 2026-11-20 |
| DEC-080 | H743 VBUS sense divider (IF-022; P1-006; pin allocation F-06) | R603 5k1 (C105872) from `VBUS` to `USB_FS_VBUS` and R604 10k (C144807) from `USB_FS_VBUS` to `GND`, with C216 100 nF at PA9 (C217 in the 14:21 save; references swapped in the 14:29 save), as drawn in the 2026-11-20 14:21 save; replaces the 0 Ω direct link | Human owner: "ok i will add divider" (2026-11-20), then drew the 5k1 / 10k pair Claude proposed from values already in the BOM. PA9 is FT_u; its input maximum is Min(Min(VDD, VDDA, VDD33USB, VBAT) + 4.0, 6 V), so 4 V with VDD at 0, and above 4 V the internal pull-up or pull-down must be off (DS12110 Rev 11 Table 11 and footnote 4); AN4879 §2.6: "it is mandatory to reduce the voltage on PA9 below 4 V" (both via NotebookLM, `VERIFIED`). VBUS also powers `+3V3`, so PA9 saw 5 V before VDD at every USB power-up. With VBUS 4.75 to 5.25 V and ±1 % resistors, PA9 is 3.12 to 3.50 V with the PX4 pull-down off and 2.81 to 3.28 V with it on (RPD 30 to 50 kΩ, Table 50), against the 4.0 V limit at VDD 0 and VIH min 0.7 × VDD = 2.52 V at VDD 3.6 V (`ESTIMATED`). Software: PA9 as a plain input with no pull-down and `VBDEN` off is preferred, not required (fmu-v6c sets `GPIO_PULLDOWN`). Bench check of PA9 and USB detection open. | 2026-11-20 |

| OPEN-026 | Reset follow-ups after DEC-046 (one shared reset with one button, 2026-11-14). Open: the reset button is drawn (B? on `user-interface`, 22:19 save; drawn, not decided), and its part behind LCSC C2689510 is not checked; whether an internal reset of either MCU (watchdog, software or debugger) drives its NRST pin low (`NOT_VERIFIED` in the ST datasheets): if it does, an H743 reset also resets the F100, against spec 4.4 (no H743 control of F100 NRST) and SAF-001 (the F100 holds the servo failsafe when the H743 stops). Nothing holds NRST low until `+3V3` is good (only each MCU's internal POR). `RESET_B` was on H743 debug connector pin 4 (22:19 save), so a debugger that pulled NRST low also reset the F100; in the 2026-11-17 15:41 save it is on no debug pin, and there is no separate NRST pad (TST-001, OPEN-029). 2026-11-19 12:49 save: B901 moved to `SW_Tactile_PTS815SJM250` with `RESET_B` on pins 1 and 2 and `GND` on 3 and 4. If the PTS815 joins a different pad pair inside (for example 1 and 3), `RESET_B` is tied to `GND` and both MCUs stay in reset; the old symbol had the same risk for the other pairing. Resolved: the PTS815SJM250SMTRLFS joins pins 1 and 2, and 3 and 4 (human owner, 2026-11-19: "PTS815SJM250SMTRLFS pin 1 and 2 are join, 3 and 4 are join"), so the 12:49 map is right; the earlier `SW_Tactile_4SMD` map shorted `RESET_B` to `GND` through pins 1 and 2. Footprint pad numbers against the datasheet pin numbers: `ASSUMED`. 2026-11-20, reference manuals via NotebookLM (RM0041 added to notebook `STM32F100` and RM0433 to `STM32H743VI` with the human owner's approval; cited text): every internal reset drives NRST low for at least 20 µs. F100: RM0041 §6.1.2 and Figure 7, "The pulse generator guarantees a minimum reset pulse duration of 20 µs for each internal reset source" (WWDG, IWDG, power, software and low-power resets). H743: RM0433 §8.4.2, Figure 43 and Table 55, internal resets (POR, BOR, IWDG1, WWDG1, SFTRESET) are "propagated to the NRST pin to reset the connected external devices", 20 µs minimum. That is far above the 300 ns "not filtered" minimum of the other MCU (DocID16455 Table 37, DS12110 Table 55), so on one shared net either MCU resets the other (`VERIFIED`). 2026-11-20 13:01 save (not committed; drawn, not decided): NRST split into `H7_RESET_B` (U201 pin 14, C209) and `F1_RESET_B` (U301 pin 7, C308), each joined to `RESET_B` (B901, TP605) through a Schottky diode: D201 and D301 BAT54XV2T1G (SOD-523, LCSC C81572, new project symbol `0_Diode:D_Schottky`), anode on the NRST net, cathode on `RESET_B` (`VERIFIED`, `kicad-cli` netlist; the 12:56 save had both reversed, so B901 reset nothing). B901 or TP605 still resets both MCUs; an internal reset of one no longer reaches the other. NRST low through a diode is about 0.3 V (`ESTIMATED`; BAT54 forward drop not checked), below VIL(NRST) max 0.8 V (F100, DocID16455 Table 37) and 0.3 VDD (H743, DS12110 Table 50) (`VERIFIED`). D201 and D301 pad orientation checked by the human owner ("BAT54XV2T1G checked", 2026-11-20). Decided 2026-11-20: the diode split (DEC-074). Checked 2026-11-21: LCSC C2689510 is C&K PTS815SJM250SMTRLFS, SMD-4P 4.2 x 3.2 mm, 1.8 N (180 gf) operating force (`VERIFIED`, lcsc.com product page, read by Claude); the footprint has pads 1 and 2 in one 4.5 mm row and pads 3 and 4 in the other, and the human owner confirmed that 1-2 and 3-4 are the joined pairs (2026-11-21), so the footprint pad numbers match. Still open: a bench check that B901 resets both MCUs and an F100 reset leaves the H743 running. 2026-11-21: only the bench check is left, now in P4-002. | Servo outputs could drop during an H743 reset; fixed by DEC-074 if the bench check passes. | Hardware / P4 (P4-002) |

| OPEN-034 | Checks from DEC-053 and DEC-054 (2026-11-19). (1) FS-iA6B: PPM output level, pin order, supply current (50 mA `ASSUMED` in the 5 V budget) and its PPM output on link loss. px4io treats PPM as lost only after 200 ms with no frames (PX4 v1.16.2 `controls.cpp`), so if the receiver keeps sending frames, PX4 sees no RC loss unless the receiver failsafe sets throttle below `RC_FAILS_THR` (`ASSUMED`). (2) Tekko32 data: moved to OPEN-017. (3) SiK radio set to 920 to 925 MHz under the local radio rules (team). (4) RC IN drawn in the 2026-11-19 15:03 save (P2-023): CN706, D707, R714, U303 and `RCIN_TIM1_CH1` to PA8; differences in item (6). (5) Team proposal (2026-11-19, not decided): replace the 6.2 kΩ / 10 kΩ divider with a 74LVC1G17 Schmitt-trigger buffer (SOT-23-5 or SC-70-5) on `+3V3` with 100 nF: CN706 pin 1, ESD, 1 kΩ series, input A with 100 kΩ to `GND`, output Y, 22 Ω, PA8. PA8 then sees a 3.3 V push-pull level from a 3.3 V or 5 V receiver, so the F-21 pull-up rule holds without measuring the PPM level, and with no receiver PA8 stays low and px4io reports RC loss. Cost: one IC, one capacitor and one more resistor, and a new library part. To check (`NOT_VERIFIED`, no NotebookLM notebook for the part yet): 5.5 V input tolerance at VCC 3.3 V, VT+ max at VCC 3.0 V, Ioff, LCSC stock. Claude review adds: buffer VCC on the same rail as the F100 VDD, and the ESD working voltage against a 5 V PPM high. The buffer does not remove the link-loss check in (1) or P4-016. Decided 2026-11-19: buffer (DEC-056). Still open: the part checks above, its maker and package, and a new library part (`kicad-part-start`). (6) RC IN as drawn (15:03 save; drawn, not decided) differs from DEC-053 and DEC-056 (`VERIFIED`, CAD): (a) CN706 is a 3-pin JST-GH 1.25 mm (SM03B-GHS-TB footprint) with pin 1 `+5V`, pin 2 signal, pin 3 `GND`, not the 2.54 mm servo header with pin 1 PPM, so the FS-iA6B servo cable needs an adapter cable (MAJOR); (b) U303 is an SN74LVC1G34 buffer with no input hysteresis, not the 74LVC1G17 Schmitt trigger (MINOR); (c) R714 is 22 Ω, not the 1 kΩ series resistor (MINOR); (d) D707 SP3031-01E: its Description gives 5 V max working voltage, against a 5 V PPM high up to 5.25 V (`NOT_VERIFIED`, datasheet not read; MINOR); (e) CN706 has no MPN, MFR or LCSC field and D707 no LCSC field (INFO). 15:20 save: (e) done (CN706 JST SM03B-GHS-TB(LF)(SN), C514175; diode C207283); the diode is now SP3031-01ETG, Description "5V(Vswr)", so (d) stays, and it is not annotated (`D?`, preflight; MINOR). 15:25 save: annotated D707 again. Decided 2026-11-19 (DEC-057): (b) U303 SN74LVC1G34, (c) R714 22 Ω and (d) D707 SP3031-01ETG are accepted as drawn. Since the 2026-11-19 23:17 save the RC IN ESD diode is D708 (same part, C207283) and D707 sits on the GPS1 buzzer line (OPEN-023). Item (d) closed: the SP3031-01ETG data is checked (CMP-012) and DEC-071 accepts its 5.0 V VRWM against the PPM high up to 5.25 V. Still open: (a) CN706 type and pin order; the human owner decides it with the team later (2026-11-19). To settle: amend DEC-053 and DEC-056 to the drawing, or redraw. The item (5) part checks apply to the SN74LVC1G34 too. 16:03 save: the RC IN 22 Ω series resistor is now R715 (DEC-057 names it R714). 2026-11-20 save (drawn, not decided): CN706 is now HX PZ2.54-1x3P WZ (LCSC C32713262), a 1x3 2.54 mm horizontal header, so the connector type matches DEC-053; the pin order is unchanged (1 `+5V`, 2 signal, 3 `GND`), against pin 1 PPM and pin 2 `+5V` in IF-038 and P2-023 (`VERIFIED`, `kicad-cli` netlist). A servo lead in signal, +, - order (`ASSUMED` for the FS-iA6B) now plugs in directly and puts the board `+5V` on the receiver signal pin, or on its GND pin if reversed (MAJOR). Item (a) closed 2026-11-20: pin order accepted as drawn (DEC-072). 2026-11-21: SN74LVC1G34 (U303): "Inputs accept voltages to 5.5V" and "Ioff supports live insertion, partial power down mode, and back drive protection", VCC 1.65 to 5.5 V (ti.com product page, `VERIFIED`); no input hysteresis is stated. ## Current verdict

**Draft only. NOT_VERIFIED and NOT FOR FABRICATION.**

The specification supports schematic capture against the agreed pinmap (REF-07). It approves no part number outside the part-number boundary (top of this document), and no power, safety-timing, schematic, PCB or fabrication release. The sheets are in capture and drawn, not decided (4.2 and 4.3 "Schematic state"); ERC on the whole schematic (2026-11-20 13:37 run by the human owner, `ERC.rpt`): 0 errors, 0 warnings; Claude's `kicad-cli` ERC on the 14:21 save (VBUS divider, DEC-080) gives 0 violations; an ERC export at 14:56 writes `documents/verification/AO-Horizon-erc.rpt` (0 errors, 0 warnings; new file, and `ERC.rpt` is deleted in the working tree), and Claude's `kicad-cli` ERC on the 14:57 save gives 0 violations; not a proof of correctness. Main open risks: sensor lifecycle and sourcing (OPEN-004; land patterns accepted without the TDK app notes, DEC-086); motor stop on an H743 hang depends on the ESC response to the F100 failsafe pulse, and the Tekko32 data (OPEN-017); all servo current passes through the board (OPEN-021); keeping the ESC and RC IN headers apart from the +6 V servo headers, (OPEN-031); mounting (OPEN-009); the `+3V3A` load and sensor power-up order (OPEN-011); the B901 part check and the reset bench check (OPEN-026); the HSE bypass firmware (OPEN-028, DEC-045; U302 margins accepted, DEC-089); the remaining INFO net names (OPEN-032); the FS-iA6B data and receiver lead, with the CN706 pin order kept with `+5V` on pin 1 (DEC-072, OPEN-034); the INFO reference prefixes on the new pads (OPEN-039). All open items are in 8.2.
